Compactifications of spaces of symmetric matrices and pointed Kontsevich spaces of isotropic Grassmannians
Hanlong Fang, Alex Massarenti, Xian Wu

Abstract
. We study two closely related families of varieties arising from genus 0 0 stable maps to the Lagrangian Grassmannian LG ⁡ ( n , 2 ​ n ) \operatorname{LG}(n,2n) . First, we construct the Kausz–type compactification 𝒯 ​ ℒ n \mathcal{TL}_{n} of the space of symmetric matrices and give an explicit description of its birational geometry. Second, we realize 𝒯 ​ ℒ n \mathcal{TL}_{n} as a general evaluation fiber in a Kontsevich space, and then exploit this modular interpretation to derive consequences for the birational geometry of the space of pointed conics M ¯ 0 , 1 ​ ( LG ⁡ ( n , 2 ​ n ) , 2 ) \overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2) . Analogous compactifications related to orthogonal Grassmannians are also presented.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14M27, 14E30; Secondary 14J45, 14N05, 14E07, 14M27

Introduction

Kontsevich moduli spaces of stable maps to homogeneous varieties are fundamental objects in modern algebraic geometry: they encode Gromov–Witten theory while simultaneously providing a rich testing ground for the minimal model program and the birational classification of higher–dimensional varieties.
Recently, there has been significant interest in the cohomological properties of Kontsevich spaces of rational curves with homogeneous targets. Pandharipande
[
54
]
computed the canonical classes of the moduli space

\overline{M}_{0,m}(\mathbb{P}^{n},d)
of genus
0
degree
d
stable maps with
m
marked points and determined their Picard groups
[
55
]
. Getzler and Pandharipande
[
33
]
established an algorithm to compute their Betti numbers. Furthermore, Oprea
[
53
]
showed that the rational cohomology groups of
\overline{M}_{0,m}(X,\beta)
are generated by tautological classes for any flag variety of Type A. Mustaţă–Mustaţă

[
49
,
50
,
51
]
described the rational Chow rings of
\overline{M}_{0,m}(\mathbb{P}^{n},d)
.

Coskun and Starr
[
20
]
initiated a systematic study of divisor classes on the unpointed Kontsevich moduli spaces of rational curves in Grassmannians. Coskun, Harris and Starr
[
18
,
19
]
described the divisor cones of several Kontsevich spaces of low degree via explicit contractions and flips. Chen
[
15
]
carried out a Mori–theoretic analysis of

\overline{M}_{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^{3},3)
. Chen and Coskun
[
13
]
fully described the birational geometry of spaces of conics and cubics in Grassmannians. Their description has been taken up and further developed by Chung and Moon
[
16
]
, and related to the theory of complete quadrics by Lozano Huerta
[
42
]
. In the isotropic setting relevant here, the unpointed conics in
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
and its divisor theory were analyzed in
[
17
]
.

Despite the central role that marked points play in Gromov–Witten theory, relatively few works offer an explicit and uniform birational description of pointed Kontsevich spaces that would allow for a systematic derivation of their algebro-geometric and complex geometric invariants–such as Fano thresholds, Fano indices, and automorphism groups.
One notable distinction from the unpointed setting is that unpointed Kontsevich spaces often admit explicit birational transformations to various alternative compactifications, including those arising from GIT quotients, Hilbert schemes, Chow varieties, or specific spherical varieties such as the De Concini–Procesi wonderful compactifications
[
37
,
13
,
14
,
42
,
16
,
17
,
12
]
. These compactifications serve as powerful tools for studying divisor theory. In contrast, the situation for pointed ones is less transparent; for instance,
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(2,4),2)
is not a spherical variety under natural group actions.

The theory of wonderful compactifications constitutes a cornerstone in the study of homogeneous varieties with various applications in algebraic geometry, representation theory, algebraic statistics
[
24
,
59
,
43
,
22
,
45
]
. Nevertheless, the framework fails for homogeneous spaces whose automorphism groups possess a nontrivial center. The development of a more general framework is still an enticing open question, while a major step forward has been taken by Kausz
[
36
]
, who constructed a novel compactification for general linear groups. Recently,
[
26
]
realized the Kausz compactifications as blow–ups of Grassmannians
G(n,2n)
and interpreted them as universal families of the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
Hilbert quotients of Grassmannians. Thaddeus
[
60
]
proved that the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
Hilbert quotients of Grassmannians, Lagrangian Grassmannians, and orthogonal Grassmannians are isomorphic, respectively, to the spaces of complete collineations, complete quadrics, and complete skew–forms. This suggests that Kausz–type compactifications may exist in the isotropic setting, work that has been fully carried out for Grassmannians in
[
26
]
.

In this paper, we investigate the algebro-geometric and complex geometric properties of pointed Kontsevich spaces targeting Lagrangian Grassmannians
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
, by constructing an analogous Kausz–type compactification in the symplectic setting, which we denote by
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
and realize as blow–ups of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
. Then, we derive a modular interpretation of
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
as a general evaluation fiber inside a suitable pointed Kontsevich space
\overline{M}_{0,k}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),d)
. A key structural feature is that
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is a toroidal spherical variety for the action of
\Sp_{2n}
, placing it within a well–developed framework in which many birational invariants admit combinatorial descriptions
[
9
,
10
,
11
]
. In our setting, this structure can be made completely explicit. Finally, we obtain concrete applications to the divisor theory of pointed conics in
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
.

Let us be more precise. Throughout, we work over the complex number field. The following theorem provides a uniform, computable description of the birational geometry of
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
, which is a synthesis of Theorems
2.13
,
3.6
,
3.18
,
3.23
,
3.31
, Propositions
2.24
,
3.14
,
3.34
, and Corollary
3.5
:

Theorem A
.

Fix
n\geq 2
. Let
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
be the iterated blow–up of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
constructed by starting with a transverse pair of Lagrangians
p^{\pm}\in\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
and blowing up, in a prescribed order, the strict transforms of the osculating loci at
p^{+}
and
p^{-}
. Let
\mathrm{R}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\longrightarrow\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
be the blow–up morphism, and
D_{i}^{\pm}
(
0\leq i\leq n-2
) the exceptional divisors of the successive blow–ups over
p^{\pm}
. Write
H:=(\mathrm{R}_{n})^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)}(1)
. Then the following holds.

(1)

\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is a smooth projective toroidal spherical variety for the natural action of the stabilizer of
(p^{+},p^{-})
inside the symplectic group
\Sp_{2n}
. In particular,
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is a Mori dream space.

(2)

There is an equivariant flat morphism to the space of complete quadrics
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
:

(0.1)

\mathrm{P}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CQ}_{n}.

Moreover,
\mathrm{P}_{n}
realizes the universal family over the Hilbert quotient of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
under the standard
\mathbb{C}^{*}
–action in the sense of
[
3
,
35
]
.

(3)

\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
is free with standard
\mathbb{Z}
–basis

\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})\ \cong\ \mathbb{Z}\!\cdot H\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-2}\mathbb{Z}\!\cdot D_{i}^{+}\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-2}\mathbb{Z}\!\cdot D_{i}^{-}.

(4)

The effective cone is the rational polyhedral cone

\operatorname{Eff}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})=\bigl\langle D_{0}^{+},\dots,D_{n-2}^{+},D_{n-1}^{+},\,D_{0}^{-},\dots,D_{n-2}^{-},D_{n-1}^{-}\bigr\rangle,

where
D_{n-1}^{\pm}
are two further boundary divisors besides the exceptional divisors
D_{i}^{\pm}
(
0\leq i\leq n-2
), whose numerical classes are

D_{n-1}^{+}\ \equiv\ H-\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}(n-i)\,D_{i}^{+},\qquad D_{n-1}^{-}\ \equiv\ H-\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}(n-i)\,D_{i}^{-}.

(5)

For integers
p,q
such that
0\leq p,q\leq n-1
and
p+q\leq n
, set

(0.2)

N_{p,q}:=H-\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}(p-i)\,D_{i}^{+}-\sum_{i=0}^{q-1}(q-i)\,D_{i}^{-}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{TL}_{n}),

where empty sums are understood as
0
. Then
\operatorname{Nef}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
is a rational polyhedral cone, and its extremal rays are
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}N_{p,q}
:

\operatorname{Nef}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})=\bigl\langle N_{p,q}\,\big|\,0\leq p,q\leq n-1,\,\,p+q\leq n\bigr\rangle.

The Cox ring
\Cox(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
is generated by the canonical sections of the boundary divisors
D_{i}^{\pm}
(
0\leq i\leq n-1
) together with the
\Sp_{2n}
–modules of sections corresponding to the colors.

(6)

The variety
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is weak Fano for every
n\geq 2
, and Fano if and only if
n=2
. Moreover, for every
n\geq 2
, the higher cohomology groups of the tangent bundle vanish:

H^{k}\bigl(\mathcal{TL}_{n},T_{\mathcal{TL}_{n}}\bigr)=0,\,\,\,\forall\,k>0.

In particular,
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is locally rigid, namely, it admits no nontrivial local deformations.

(7)

The group of pseudoautomorphisms is given by

\operatorname{PsAut}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})=\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})\cong\ (\operatorname{GL}_{n}/\{\pm I\})\rtimes S_{2},

where
S_{2}
is the symmetric group on
2
elements.

In addition, in Propositions
3.9
and
3.11
, we determine explicit generators for the Mori cone
\NE(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
and for the cone of moving curves
\operatorname{Mov}_{1}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
. Furthermore, in Proposition
3.36
we provide an algorithm that computes the extremal generators of the movable cone of divisors
\operatorname{Mov}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
. We implemented these constructions in a collection of
Maple
scripts: given
n
as input, the code returns the generators of
\operatorname{Eff}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
,
\operatorname{Nef}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
,
\operatorname{Mov}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
,
\NE(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
, and
\operatorname{Mov}_{1}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
. See Remark
3.41
for implementation details. Examples for
n=2
and
n=3
are collected in Examples
3.37
and
3.38
.

Our investigation of the pointed Kontsevich spaces begins by identifying
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
as a general evaluation fiber in a Kontsevich space. Recall that Michałek, Monin, and Wiśniewski
[
48
, Corollary 3.11]
realized the space of complete quadrics
as the connected component of the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
–fixed locus of the Kontsevich space
\overline{M}_{0,0}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),n)
, which parallels the constructions of Thaddeus
[
60
]
. Using Theorem
A
(2) and the geometric properties of its fibers established in Lemma
2.23
, we lift such subvarieties in
\overline{M}_{0,0}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),n)
to
\overline{M}_{0,k}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),n)
with
k\leq 3
. This identification is summarized by Theorems
4.7
,
4.12
, Proposition
4.14
, and Remark
4.16
, as follows:

Theorem B
.

Consider the evaluation morphism at the first two markings

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:=\ \operatorname{ev}_{1}\times\operatorname{ev}_{2}\colon\overline{M}_{0,3}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),n)\longrightarrow\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\times\operatorname{LG}(n,2n).

For a general pair
(p,q)\in\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\times\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
, the fiber of
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}
over
(p,q)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
. Moreover,
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),n)
contains an open dense subset covered by subvarieties isomorphic to
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
.

Theorem
B
suggests using the features of
\mathcal{TL}_{d}
to probe the birational geometry of
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(d,2d),d)
, an approach that, via the embedding of the Lagrangian Grassmannians, also applies to
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),d)
with
n\geq d
. To illustrate this principle, we investigate the birational geometry of
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
, the moduli space of pointed conics in
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
.

We denote by
H_{1}:=\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}H
the pullback of the Plücker hyperplane class via the evaluation morphism at the marked point, and by
\Delta_{1|1}
the boundary divisor parametrizing reducible conics. The unbalanced divisor
D_{\unb}
is defined as the closure of the locus of conics that are double covers of a line when
n=2
; for
n\geq 3
, it is the closure of the locus of conics whose associated trivial rank
(n-2)
subbundle meets a fixed
(n+1)
–dimensional subspace in
\mathbb{P}^{2n-1}
.
On the unpointed space
\overline{M}_{0,0}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
, two natural geometric divisor classes play a central role:
the class
H_{\sigma_{2}}
, defined by imposing incidence with a general codimension–
2
Schubert cycle, and the class
T
, defined by the tangency condition that the image conic be tangent to a general Plücker hyperplane section. We view
H_{\sigma_{2}}
and
T
on
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
via pullback under the forgetful morphism.

Using the geometry of
\mathcal{TL}_{2}
together with the computations for the unpointed spaces, we obtain a complete description of the cones of divisors on
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
. The following result collects Theorems
5.12
,
5.13
, Proposition
5.14
, and Corollaries
5.15
,
5.16
.

Theorem C
.

Let
n\geq 2
. The effective and nef cone of
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
are given by

\operatorname{Eff}(\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2))=\bigl\langle H_{1},\,\Delta_{1|1},\,D_{\unb}\bigr\rangle\,\,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,\,\operatorname{Nef}(\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2))=\bigl\langle H_{1},\,H_{\sigma_{2}},\,T\bigr\rangle.

The anticanonical divisor of
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\mathrm{LG}(n,2n),2)
is given by

-K_{\overline{M}_{0,1}(\mathrm{LG}(n,2n),2)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcll}H_{1}+\frac{5}{2}H_{\sigma_{2}}+\frac{3}{4}\Delta_{1|1}&\equiv&H_{1}+H_{\sigma_{2}}+\frac{3}{2}T&\text{if }n=2\\
H_{1}+\frac{n+2}{2}H_{\sigma_{2}}+\frac{6-n}{4}\Delta_{1|1}&\equiv&H_{1}+(n-2)H_{\sigma_{2}}+\frac{6-n}{2}T&\text{if }n\geq 3\end{array}\right..

Furthermore,
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
is a Mori dream space for all
n\geq 2
.
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\mathrm{LG}(n,2n),2)
is a Fano variety if and only if
2\leq n\leq 5
, in which case the Fano index is
1
; it is weak Fano if and only if
2\leq n\leq 6
. The automorphism group is

\operatorname{Aut}(\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2))\cong\PSp_{2n},\,\,\forall\,n\geq 2.

Theorem
C
, to our knowledge, provides one of the few uniform descriptions of the birational cones of a family of pointed Kontsevich spaces targeting a higher–rank homogeneous variety. We believe that broader classes of pointed Kontsevich spaces may be accessible by analogous compactifications in other types, arising as general evaluation fibers. As a step in this direction,
we extend the Kausz–type compactification construction to orthogonal Grassmannians in the final section. In particular, we obtain the following (Theorems
6.7
,
6.8
,
6.9
,
6.10
):

Corollary D
.

All three Hilbert quotients in
[
60
]
have smooth universal families. These universal families are weak Fano varieties with vanishing higher cohomology of the tangent bundle. In particular, they are all locally rigid.

Organization of the paper

In Section
1
, we fix notation and recall fundamental results.
In Sections
2
and
3
we develop the geometry of the Kausz–type compactifications
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
and prove Theorem
A
.
Section
4
is devoted to the modular interpretation of
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
via evaluation fibers and the proof of Theorem
B
.
Section
5
applies this viewpoint to the divisor theory of
\overline{M}_{0,1}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),2)
, culminating in Theorem
C
. In Section
6
, we introduce the compactification
\mathcal{TO}_{n}
related to the orthogonal Grassmannians, and prove Corollary
D
.

Notation and conventions

We work over
\mathbb{C}
. We set
V:=W\oplus W^{\vee}
equipped with the standard symplectic form
\omega
, and we write

\operatorname{LG}(n,2n):=\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
for the Lagrangian Grassmannian.

•

The distinguished points
p_{\pm}\in\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
are as in (
2.7
).

•

\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
denotes the space of complete quadrics, and
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is the Kausz–type compactification considered in this paper.

•

\mathrm{R}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\to\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
and
\mathrm{P}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\to\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
are the natural
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant morphisms ((
2.5
) and (
0.1
)).

•

Z_{k}^{\pm}\subset\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
are the osculating loci defined from
p_{\pm}
, and
D_{k}^{\pm}\subset\mathcal{TL}_{n}
are the corresponding exceptional divisors after the iterated blow-ups (Section
2
).

•

H\in\operatorname{Pic}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n))
is the Plücker hyperplane class; for pointed moduli spaces we also use
H_{1}:=\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}H
.

•

\mathscr{G}\subset\operatorname{Aut}(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n))
is the stabilizer of the ordered pair
(p_{+},p_{-})
.

•

\mathcal{S}
denotes the tautological rank
n
subbundle on
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
; we will use the homogeneous identification

T_{\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)}\simeq\Sym^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{\vee})
.

1.
Preliminaries

We fix basic notation for divisor cones and Mori dream spaces; standard references are

[
34
,
2
,
11
,
57
]
.
For a normal projective variety
X
we write
N^{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}
for the Néron–Severi space and denote by

\operatorname{Eff}(X)
,
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, and
\operatorname{Nef}(X)
the effective, movable, and nef cones in
N^{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}
.
If
f\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
is a small
\mathbb{Q}
–factorial modification (SQM), then the induced map

f^{*}\colon N^{1}(X^{\prime})_{\mathbb{R}}\to N^{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}
is an isomorphism and it identifies
\operatorname{Eff}
and
\operatorname{Mov}
. Write
N_{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}
for the real vector space of
1
–cycles on
X
modulo the numerical equivalence.

Assume that the divisor class group
\Cl(X)
is free and finitely generated, and choose a subgroup

G\subset\Div(X)
mapping isomorphically onto
\Cl(X)
.
The
Cox ring
of
X
is

\Cox(X):=\bigoplus_{[D]\in\Cl(X)}H^{0}\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\bigr),

with multiplication induced by multiplication of rational sections.

Definition 1.1
.

A normal projective
\mathbb{Q}
–factorial variety
X
is a
Mori dream space
(MDS) if:

(i)

\operatorname{Pic}(X)
is finitely generated;

(ii)

\operatorname{Nef}(X)
is a rational polyhedral cone generated by finitely many semiample classes;

(iii)

there are finitely many SQMs
f_{i}\colon X\dasharrow X_{i}
such that each
X_{i}
satisfies (ii) and

\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\bigcup_{i}f_{i}^{*}\bigl(\operatorname{Nef}(X_{i})\bigr).

Equivalently, the nef cones of the SQMs glue to a fan supported on
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, which extends canonically to a fan on
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
X
be a Mori dream space.
The
Mori chamber decomposition
of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is the fan whose maximal cones are

\mathcal{C}_{i}:=\big\langle g_{i}^{*}\bigl(\operatorname{Nef}(Y_{i})\bigr),\;\Exc(g_{i})\big\rangle\subset N^{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}},

where
g_{i}\colon X\dasharrow Y_{i}
runs over the finitely many birational contractions to Mori dream spaces and

\Exc(g_{i})
denotes the set of
g_{i}
–exceptional prime divisors on
X
.

Remark 1.3
.

By
[
34
, Proposition 2.9]
, for a normal
\mathbb{Q}
–factorial projective variety
X
the Cox ring is finitely generated
if and only if
X
is a Mori dream space. See also
[
2
, Section 1.3]
.

Definition 1.4
.

An irreducible curve
C
on a
\mathbb{Q}
–factorial projective variety
X
is movable
if there exists a flat family of curves

\pi\colon\mathscr{C}\to T
over an irreducible base together with a morphism

\mathrm{ev}\colon\mathscr{C}\to X
such that:

-

for a general
t\in T
, the fiber
\mathscr{C}_{t}
is an irreducible curve
and
\mathrm{ev}(\mathscr{C}_{t})
is numerically equivalent to
C
;

-

the evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}
is dominant.

The cone of moving curves is the closed convex cone

\operatorname{Mov}_{1}(X)\ :=\ \overline{\bigl\langle[C]\ \big|\ C\subset X\ \text{movable}\bigr\rangle}\ \subset\ N_{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}.

Remark 1.5
.

If
X
is smooth and projective, then
[
6
, Theorem 2.2]
identifies

\operatorname{Mov}_{1}(X)
with the dual of the pseudoeffective cone of divisors:

\operatorname{Mov}_{1}(X)\ =\ \overline{\operatorname{Eff}}(X)^{\vee}\ :=\ \bigl\{\alpha\in N_{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}\ \big|\ \alpha\cdot E\geq 0\ \text{for every }E\in\operatorname{Eff}(X)\bigr\}.

Definition 1.6
.

Let
X
be a normal projective variety.

(i)

We say that
X
is
Fano
if
-K_{X}
is ample.

(ii)

We say that
X
is
weak Fano
if
-K_{X}
is nef and big.

(iii)

A
log Fano pair
is a pair
(X,\Delta)
where
X
is normal projective,
\Delta
is an effective
\mathbb{Q}
–divisor,

(X,\Delta)
has klt singularities, and
-(K_{X}+\Delta)
is ample.

Remark 1.7
.

By
[
5
, Corollary 1.3.2]
, every log Fano pair is a Mori dream space.
In particular, a Fano or weak Fano variety is a Mori dream space after adding a suitable effective boundary.

For a
\mathbb{Q}
–Gorenstein Fano variety
X
, the Fano index is defined as

\iota(X):=\max\bigl\{m\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}\,\big|\,-K_{X}=mH\ \text{for some ample Cartier divisor }H\in\operatorname{Pic}(X)\,\bigr\},

equivalently, the largest positive integer dividing
-K_{X}
in
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
.

We denote by
\operatorname{PsAut}(\mathcal{TL}_{n})
the group of pseudoautomorphisms of
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
, namely, birational
self–maps which are isomorphisms in codimension
1
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a connected reductive algebraic group and let
\mathscr{B}\subset\mathscr{G}
be a Borel subgroup.

Definition 1.8
.

A normal irreducible
\mathscr{G}
–variety
X
is
spherical
if it contains an open dense
\mathscr{B}
–orbit.

Definition 1.9
.

A smooth projective spherical
\mathscr{G}
–variety
X
is
wonderful
if it has a unique closed
\mathscr{G}
–orbit and the boundary

X\setminus\mathscr{G}\cdot x
is a divisor with normal crossings whose strata are exactly the
\mathscr{G}
–orbit closures.

Definition 1.10
.

Let
X
be a spherical
\mathscr{G}
–variety with a fixed Borel subgroup
\mathscr{B}
. The boundary divisors of
X
are its
\mathscr{G}
–stable prime divisors. The colors of
X
are its
\mathscr{B}
–stable but not
\mathscr{G}
–stable prime divisors.

Remark 1.11
.

Spherical varieties are Mori dream spaces; the Mori chamber decomposition can be read from the combinatorics of colored fans.
We refer to
[
57
, Section 4]
for a convenient overview and further references.

Definition 1.12
.

A normal spherical
\mathscr{G}
–variety
X
is called toroidal if no color contains a
\mathscr{G}
–orbit closure in
X
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a semisimple group of adjoint type. Denoted by
\overline{\mathscr{G}}
its De Concini–Procesi wonderful compactification.
Then, from the point of view above,
\overline{\mathscr{G}}
is a smooth projective spherical
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–variety,
its boundary divisors come from
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–stable and
\mathscr{B}\times\mathscr{B}
–stable degeneracy conditions, and

\overline{\mathscr{G}}
is therefore a Mori dream space with Cox ring and Mori chamber decomposition governed by the boundary divisors and colors.

When
\mathscr{G}
is reductive but not of adjoint type, the wonderful compactification does not exist. While a general framework as simple as the De Concini-Procesi construction is lacking, Kausz constructed a beautiful compactification for general linear groups. We summarize certain important properties of Kausz compactifications as follows.

Theorem 1.13

(
[
36
]
and
[
26
]
)
.

Fix
\mathscr{G}:=\operatorname{GL}_{n}
.

\overline{\mathscr{G}}
is a smooth projective
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–spherical variety. The complement of the open

\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–orbit in
\overline{\mathscr{G}}
consists of
2n
smooth prime divisors with simple normal crossings
D^{+}_{1},\cdots,D^{+}_{n},D^{-}_{1}
,
\cdots,D^{-}_{n}
such that the following holds.

(A)

D^{+}_{1}\cong D^{-}_{1}\cong\mathsf{CC}_{n}
, where
\mathsf{CC}_{n}
is the space of complete collineations.

(B)

There is a
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–equivariant flat retraction

\mathrm{P}_{n}:\overline{\mathscr{G}}\longrightarrow D_{1}^{-}\cong\mathsf{CC}_{n}

such that the restriction
\mathrm{P}_{n}|_{D^{+}_{1}}:D_{1}^{+}\rightarrow D_{1}^{-}
is an isomorphism and that for
2\leq i\leq r
,

(1.14)

\mathrm{P}_{n}(D^{-}_{i})=\mathrm{P}_{n}(D^{+}_{n+2-i})=:\check{D}_{i}\,\,

(C)

The closures of
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–orbits in
\overline{\mathscr{G}}
are one-to-one given by

(1.15)

\bigcap\nolimits_{\,\,i\in I^{+}}D^{+}_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.1}{$\displaystyle\bigcap$}}{\scalebox{1.1}{$\textstyle\bigcap$}}{\scalebox{1.1}{$\scriptstyle\bigcap$}}{\scalebox{1.1}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcap$}}}\bigcap\nolimits_{\,\,i\in I^{-}}D^{-}_{i}

for subsets
I^{+},I^{-}\subset\{1,2,\cdots,n\}

such that
\min(I^{+})+\min(I^{-})\geq n+2
with the convention that
\min(\emptyset)=+\infty
.

(D)

D_{1}^{-}\cong\mathsf{CC}_{n}
is wonderful with the
\mathscr{G}\times\mathscr{G}
–stable divisors
\check{D}_{i}
,
2\leq i\leq n
.

Notice that
\overline{\mathscr{G}}
is spherical but not of wonderful type in the strict sense of De Concini–Procesi. In particular, it is not a simple spherical variety: indeed, certain pairs of boundary components do not intersect; for instance
D_{0}^{-}\cap D_{0}^{+}=\emptyset
.
Consequently, and in contrast to the case of simple spherical varieties treated in
[
7
]
, the divisor theory may not be well described by the color fans.

2.
Kausz–Type Compactifications of Spaces of Symmetric Matrices

Let
V
be a complex vector space of dimension
2n
endowed with a nondegenerate symplectic form
\omega
. We denote by
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
the Lagrangian Grassmannian parametrising Lagrangian subspaces of
V
, namely, maximal isotropic subspaces of dimension
n
. Then
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)\cong\Sp(V,\omega)/P
is a rational homogeneous variety of type
C_{n}
, where
P
denotes the maximal parabolic subgroup associated to the cominuscule root
\alpha_{n}
in the Dynkin diagram

\overset{\alpha_{1}}{\circ}-\overset{\alpha_{2}}{\circ}-\cdots-\overset{\alpha_{n-1}}{\circ}\Leftarrow\overset{\alpha_{n}}{\circ}.

In particular,
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
is smooth projective variety of dimension
\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
,
\operatorname{Pic}(\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega))\cong\mathbb{Z}
is generated by an ample line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)}(1)
, and the Fano index of
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
is
n+1
, namely, the anticanonical class
-K_{\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)}=(n+1)\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)}(1)
.
Moreover,
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
is a spherical
\Sp(V,\omega)
–variety.

We now introduce the Kausz–type compactification of the open linear orbit inside
\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)
. Let
W
be an
n
–dimensional complex vector space and set
V:=W\oplus W^{\vee}
with its canonical symplectic form

\omega((w,\varphi),(w^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})):=\varphi^{\prime}(w)-\varphi(w^{\prime}),\qquad w,w^{\prime}\in W,\ \varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in W^{\vee}.

Henceforth, we omit
\omega
from the notation and denote the Lagrangian Grassmannian by
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
. Let

\mathscr{G}:=\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\subset\Sp(V)

be the subgroup defined as follows:
g\in\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})
acts on
W^{\vee}
by the given representation
and on
W=(W^{\vee})^{\vee}
by the contragredient representation, while

\lambda\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts with weight
+1
on
W
and weight
-1
on

W^{\vee}
. Explicitly,

(2.1)

(g,\lambda)\cdot(w,\varphi):=\bigl(\lambda(g^{-1})^{\!\top}w,\ \lambda^{-1}g\varphi\bigr),\qquad w\in W,\ \varphi\in W^{\vee},

where
(g^{-1})^{\!\top}\colon W\to W
is the transpose inverse of
g
.
Based on the Plücker embedding induced by
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)}(1)

(2.2)

e_{\operatorname{LG}}\colon\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\bigwedge^{n}(W\oplus W^{\vee})\big),

we define a rational
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant map

(2.3)

\mathrm{KL}_{n}\colon\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\bigwedge^{n}(W\oplus W^{\vee})\big)\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}W^{\vee}\big)\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}\big(\bigwedge^{2}W^{\vee}\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}\big(\bigwedge^{n}W^{\vee}\big)\big),

sending a general Lagrangian subspace to a compatible collection of symmetric forms obtained by taking appropriate minors of its Plücker coordinates.
Following
[
26
]
, we make the next definition.

Definition 2.4
.

The
Kausz–type compactification

\mathcal{TL}_{n}
of the open
\mathscr{G}
–orbit in
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
is defined as the closure of the image of
\mathrm{KL}_{n}
:

\mathcal{TL}_{n}:=\overline{\mathrm{KL}_{n}\big(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\big)}\subset\mathbb{P}\big(\bigwedge^{n}(W\oplus W^{\vee})\big)\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}W^{\vee}\big)\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}\big(\bigwedge^{2}W^{\vee}\big)\big)\times\cdots\times\mathbb{P}\big(\Sym^{2}\big(\bigwedge^{n}W^{\vee}\big)\big).

We denote by

(2.5)

\mathrm{R}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\longrightarrow\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\subset\mathbb{P}\big(\bigwedge^{n}(W\oplus W^{\vee})\big)

the projection to the first factor.

Remark 2.6
.

Since
\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
– and
\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})
–orbits are identical in the above construction, we do not distinguish between treating
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
as a spherical
\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
– or
\operatorname{GL}(W^{\vee})
–variety.

Consider the following pair of complementary Lagrangian subspaces

(2.7)

p_{+}:=0\oplus W^{\vee},\,\,\,p_{-}:=W\oplus 0\,\,\,\in\,\operatorname{LG}(n,2n),

which will play symmetric roles in the paper. For each
k
with
1\leq k\leq n-1
, we denote by

\Osc^{k}_{p_{\pm}}\big(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\big)\subset\mathbb{P}\big(\bigwedge^{n}(W\oplus W^{\vee})\big)

the
k
–th osculating space at
p_{\pm}
with respect to the Plücker embedding (see
[
46
, Section 6]
and
[
29
, Section 3.4]
for detailed
descriptions of the osculating spaces of Grassmannians and Lagrangian
Grassmannians in Plücker coordinates, respectively). For later use we record the following
lemma, which packages together the behavior of the osculating spaces at a
point of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
, the loci covered by low–degree rational curves through
that point, and the dimensions of these loci.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)=\operatorname{LG}(V,\omega)\subset\mathbb{P}(\bigwedge^{n}V)

be the Lagrangian Grassmannian in its Plücker embedding.
Fix a point
p\in\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
corresponding to a Lagrangian

\Lambda_{0}\subset V
. For an integer
d
with
1\leq d\leq n-1
, set

Z_{d}(p)\ :=\ \operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\cap\Osc^{d}_{p}\big(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\big)

and let
R_{d}(p)\subset\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
be the union of all irreducible rational
curves
C\subset\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
with
p\in C
and
\deg_{H}(C)\leq d
.
Then the following hold:

(i)

As closed subsets of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
one has

Z_{d}(p)\ =\ \big\{\Lambda\in\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\,\big|\,\dim(\Lambda\cap\Lambda_{0})\geq n-d\big\}.

(ii)

The closed set
Z_{d}(p)
is the Zariski closure of
R_{d}(p)
; in
particular
Z_{d}(p)
is irreducible and

\overline{R_{d}(p)}\ =\ Z_{d}(p).

(iii)

The variety
Z_{d}(p)
has dimension

\dim Z_{d}(p)\ =\ \frac{d\big(2n-d+1\big)}{2}\ =\ dn-\frac{d(d-1)}{2}.

One has
\Osc^{d}_{p}\big(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\big)=\mathbb{P}(\bigwedge^{n}V)
and hence

Z_{d}(p)=\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
for
d\geq n
.

Proof.

By
\Sp(V,\omega)
–homogeneity we may assume
\Lambda_{0}=W\oplus 0\subset W\oplus W^{\vee}=V
.
The big cell of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
consisting of Lagrangians transverse to
0\oplus W^{\vee}
is identified with
\Sym^{2}W^{\vee}
via graphs of symmetric maps
A\colon W\to W^{\vee}
, and
p
corresponds to
A=0
.
In these coordinates,
\dim(\Lambda\cap\Lambda_{0})\geq n-d
is equivalent to
\rk(A)\leq d
, giving (i). The locus
\{\rk(A)\leq d\}
is irreducible and equals the closure of the union of degree
\leq d
rational curves through
A=0
, yielding (ii).
Finally, the standard determinantal computation for symmetric matrices gives
\dim\{\rk(A)\leq d\}=\frac{d(2n-d+1)}{2}
, which is (iii).
∎

We define

Z_{k}^{\pm}:=\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\cap\Osc^{k}_{p_{\pm}}\big(\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)\big).

Each
Z_{k}^{\pm}
is
\mathscr{G}
–stable and we have chains

Z_{0}^{+}:=\{p_{+}\}\subset Z_{1}^{+}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{n-1}^{+},\qquad Z_{0}^{-}:=\{p_{-}\}\subset Z_{1}^{-}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{n-1}^{-}.

\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)=\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{0}\xleftarrow{\ \mathrm{Bl}_{Z_{0}^{+}}\ }\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{1,+}\xleftarrow{\ \mathrm{Bl}_{Z_{1}^{+}}\ }\cdots\xleftarrow{\ \mathrm{Bl}_{Z_{n-2}^{+}}\ }\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1,+}\xleftarrow{\ \mathrm{Bl}_{Z_{0}^{-}}\ }\cdots\xleftarrow{\ \mathrm{Bl}_{Z_{n-2}^{-}}\ }\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}\cong\mathcal{TL}_{n}.

Figure 1
.
Schematic order of the iterated blow-ups along the osculating loci
Z_{k}^{\pm}
producing
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
.

Proposition 2.9
.

For every integer
k
with
1\leq k\leq n-1
the following holds:

\dim(Z_{k}^{+})=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-\frac{(n-k)(n-k+1)}{2}

and
Z_{k}^{+}
has ordinary singularities of multiplicity
\mult_{Z_{i}^{+}}(Z_{k}^{+})\ =\ k-i+1
along
Z_{i}^{+}
for every
0\leq i<k
. The analogous statement holds for the chain
Z_{0}^{-}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{n-1}^{-}
.

Let
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{k}
be obtained from
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
by sequentially blowing up the strict transforms of
Z_{0}^{+},Z_{1}^{+},\dots,Z_{k-1}^{+},Z_{0}^{-}
,
Z_{1}^{-}
,
\dots
,
Z_{k-1}^{-}
(in that order).
Then
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{k}
is smooth, and the strict transforms of
Z_{k}^{+}
and
Z_{k}^{-}
in
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{k}
are smooth. In particular,
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}
is smooth.

Proof.

The dimension formula is Lemma
2.8
(iii).

Fix
k
and work in the standard big cell
U_{+}
around
p_{+}
, identified with
\Sym^{2}W
by graphs of symmetric maps.
In these coordinates,
Z_{k}^{+}
is the symmetric determinantal locus

Z_{k}^{+}\cap U_{+}\ =\ \{A\in\Sym^{2}W:\rk(A)\leq k\},

cut out by the
(k+1)\times(k+1)
minors of
A
. It is a standard determinantal computation for symmetric matrices that along the lower rank stratum

Z_{i}^{+}\cap U_{+}=\{\rk(A)\leq i\}
the singularity is ordinary and the order of vanishing is
k-i+1
, i.e.
\mult_{Z_{i}^{+}}(Z_{k}^{+})=k-i+1
.
The analogous statement for the
-
–chain follows by working in the opposite big cell
U_{-}
around
p_{-}
.

Finally, let
x
be a point lying on (strict transforms of) both chains. After a symplectic change of basis we may assume that, in an affine chart around
x
, a Lagrangian is represented by a symmetric block matrix

\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
B^{\top}&C\end{pmatrix},\qquad A\in\Sym^{2}U,\ \ C\in\Sym^{2}U^{\prime},

for a decomposition
W=U\oplus U^{\prime}
with
\dim U=n-\ell
and
\dim U^{\prime}=\ell
.
In these coordinates the defining equations for the
+
–chain involve only minors of the block
A
, whereas the defining equations for the
-
–chain involve only minors of the block
C
; the entries of
B
give transverse coordinates.
In particular, the two chains meet cleanly and are locally defined in independent coordinates. Therefore the successive blow-ups along the strict transforms of

Z_{0}^{+},Z_{1}^{+},\dots,Z_{k-1}^{+},Z_{0}^{-},\dots,Z_{k-1}^{-}
resolve the ordinary singularities without introducing new ones, and
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{k}
as well as the strict transforms of
Z_{k}^{\pm}
are smooth.
∎

We now compare
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}
with the compactification
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
given in Definition
2.4
.

Proposition 2.10
.

There is a natural
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant
isomorphism
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}\cong\mathcal{TL}_{n}
such that the blow up morphism
\beta\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}\to\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
identifies with the projection

\mathrm{R}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\to\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
in (
2.5
).

Proof.

Set
Z_{0}^{+}:=\{p_{+}\}
and
Z_{0}^{-}:=\{p_{-}\}
.
The morphism
\mathrm{R}_{n}
is projective, birational, and an isomorphism over the locus where
\mathrm{KL}_{n}
is regular.
In Plücker coordinates one checks:

(a)

\mathrm{KL}_{n}
is regular on the complement of
\bigcup_{k=0}^{n-1}(Z_{k}^{+}\cup Z_{k}^{-})
;

(b)

for
0\leq k\leq n-2
, a general point of
Z_{k}^{\pm}
is an ordinary base point of the linear systems defining
\mathrm{KL}_{n}
, with multiplicity prescribed by Lemma
2.8
;

(c)

\mathrm{KL}_{n}
is regular at a general point of
Z_{n-1}^{\pm}
.

Therefore blowing up the centers in Proposition
2.9
resolves the indeterminacies of
\mathrm{KL}_{n}
and identifies the resulting graph compactification with
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
, yielding the claimed
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant isomorphism.
∎

Remark 2.11
.

As in
[
26
, Lemma 2.2]
, one can show that the iterated blow–up construction yielding
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is independent of the order in which the blow–ups are performed.

Definition 2.12
.

Denote by
D_{k}^{\pm}
the exceptional divisor over
Z_{k}^{\pm}
for
0\leq k\leq n-2
, and by
D_{n-1}^{\pm}

the strict transform of
Z_{n-1}^{\pm}
in
\mathcal{TL}_{n}^{n-1}\cong\mathcal{TL}_{n}
.

Theorem 2.13
.

The variety
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is smooth and projective. The group
\mathscr{G}

acts on
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
with an open dense orbit isomorphic to a homogeneous space
\mathscr{G}/\mathscr{H}
, where
\mathscr{H}\subset\mathscr{G}
is the stabilizer of a fixed nondegenerate symmetric form on
W^{\vee}
. The complement of this open orbit is a divisor with
2n
smooth irreducible components

(2.14)

D_{0}^{-},\dots,D_{n-1}^{-},\ D_{0}^{+},\dots,D_{n-1}^{+}.

In particular,
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is a smooth projective spherical
\mathscr{G}
–variety.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.10
,
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is obtained by an iterated blow–up of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
along smooth centers. Hence
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is smooth and projective. Moreover, the open dense
\mathscr{G}
–orbit is identified with the open subset of
\Sym^{2}W^{\vee}
consisting of nondegenerate symmetric forms, and the boundary divisors are given by (
2.14
). It is clear that
\mathscr{B}\cdot[p_{-}]
is an open dense
\mathscr{B}
–orbit in
\mathscr{G}/\mathscr{H}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Let
\Sym^{2}(W^{\vee})
denote the space of symmetric
n\times n
matrices and let
\mathbb{P}(\Sym^{2}W^{\vee})\cong\mathbb{P}^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1}
be its projectivization. The
space of complete quadrics
in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
can be constructed by successively blowing up the strata of symmetric matrices of rank at most
k
. We denote this smooth projective variety by
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
. Note that the first boundary divisor
D_{0}^{-}\subset\mathcal{TL}_{n}
is isomorphic to
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
, and so is
D_{0}^{+}
.

Proposition 2.15
.

There are
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant isomorphisms
D_{0}^{-}\ \cong\ \mathsf{CQ}_{n}\ \cong\ D_{0}^{+}
.

Proof.

Let
E_{0}^{+}
be the exceptional divisor of the blow up of
\operatorname{LG}(n,2n)
in
p_{+}
. Then
E_{0}^{+}\cong\mathbb{P}^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1}
. Finally, the claim follows from Proposition
2.10
and the blow–up construction of
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
in
[
61
]
.
∎

Composing the projection onto
D_{0}^{\pm}
with the identification with
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
, we can obtain a
\mathscr{G}
–equivariant flat morphism

(2.16)

\mathrm{P}_{n}\colon\mathcal{TL}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CQ}_{n}

as in
[
26
]
, whose restriction to
D_{0}^{\pm}
is an isomorphism onto
\mathsf{CQ}_{n}
.

Mille Crêpes charts on
\mathcal{TL}_{n}

Following
[
26
, Section 3]
, we shall define the Mille Crêpes coordinates for
\mathcal{TL}_{n}
, by exploiting the above iterated blow–up construction. For simplicity, we present them here in a concise, abstract form; the full details can be found in
[
25
]
.

For each
I=(i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{n})\in\{(i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}:1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{n}\leq 2n\}=:\mathbb{I}_{n}
, the
Plücker coordinate function

P_{I}
on
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[x_{ij}(1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq 2n)\right]
is the determinant of the matrix
(x_{ij})
consisting of the
i_{1}
–th,
\cdots
,
i_{n}
–th columns. Denote by
\operatorname{Pr}\colon\mathcal{G}(n,2n)\rightarrow G(n,2n)
the natural projection from the torsor

\mathcal{G}(n,2n):=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in{\rm Spec}\mathbb{Z}\left[x_{ij}\right]:P_{I}\notin\mathfrak{p}\,\,{\rm for\,\,a\,\,certain\,\,}I\in\mathbb{I}_{n}\right\}

to the Grassmannian
G(n,2n)
. For each
x\in G(n,2n)
, let
\widetilde{x}
be an element in the preimage
(\operatorname{Pr})^{-1}(x)
.

Now, for
0\leq k\leq n
, we
define index sets

\mathbb{I}_{n}^{k}:=\big\{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}:{1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{n-k}\leq n\,;n+1\leq i_{n-k+1}<\cdots<i_{n}\leq 2n}\},

and rational maps

(2.17)

\begin{split}f^{k}\colon\mathrm{LG}(n,2n)\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N^{k}_{n}},\,\,\,x\mapsto[\cdots,P_{I}(\widetilde{x}),\cdots]_{I\in\mathbb{I}^{k}_{n}}.\end{split}

Then (
2.3
) takes the form

(2.18)

\begin{split}\mathrm{KL}_{n}:=&(e_{\mathrm{LG}},f^{0},\cdots,f^{n})\colon\mathrm{LG}(n,2n)\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N_{n}}\times\mathbb{P}^{N^{0}_{n}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{P}^{N^{n}_{n}}\\
&x\mapsto\big([\cdots,P_{I}(\widetilde{x}),\cdots]_{I\in\mathbb{I}_{n}},[\cdots,P_{I}(\widetilde{x}),\cdots]_{I\in\mathbb{I}^{0}_{n}},\cdots,[\cdots,P_{I}(\widetilde{x}),\cdots]_{I\in\mathbb{I}^{n}_{n}}\big).\end{split}

Here
N_{n}:=\frac{(2n)!}{n!\cdot n!}-1
and
N_{n}^{k}:=|\mathbb{I}_{n}^{k}|-1
;
e_{\operatorname{LG}}
is the Plücker embedding in (
2.2
).

On the open subset of Lagrangian subspaces transverse to
W
every
L
is the graph of a unique linear map
A\colon W\to W^{\vee}
which is symmetric with respect to the natural pairing. Choosing a basis
\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{n}\}
for
W
and letting
\{e_{1}^{*},\cdots,e_{n}^{*}\}
denote its dual basis in
W^{\vee}
, we may thus identify this open subset with the affine space of symmetric matrices

Y=(y_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
.
More generally, for each Lagrangian subspace
L_{12\cdots\ell}
generated by
e_{\ell+1},\cdots,e_{n},e^{*}_{1},\cdots,e^{*}_{\ell}
, where
0\leq\ell\leq n
,
\operatorname{LG}(W\oplus W^{\vee})
has the following affine coordinate chart around
L_{12\cdots\ell}
:

(2.19)

U_{12\cdots\ell}:=\{\,\,\,\,\left(\;\hskip-19.5132pt\smash[b]{\underbrace{\begin{matrix}\,\,\,Z\\
\,\,\,-X^{\!\top}\end{matrix}}_{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\ell\,\,\rm columns}}\;\right.\vphantom{\underbrace{\begin{matrix}\,\,\,Z\\
\,\,\,-X^{\!\top}\end{matrix}}_{\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\ell\,\,\rm columns}}\hskip-28.90755pt\begin{matrix}&\hfill\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\\
&\hfill\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

I_{n-\ell}\\
\end{matrix}\hskip-7.94974pt\begin{matrix}&\hfill\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

&\hfill\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
